ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u105	...on the draft parliamentary law introducing a law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and a law on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. Ladies and gentlemen! A law containing the introductory provisions is a template law, standard law, customarily adopted by large acts which require quite complex implementing rules. And so it is in this case. The Act provides for a very standard, but also the most frequently adopted period of vacathio legis, providing for a 14-day date for the entry into force of both laws. Of course, exceptions are also envisaged, which are most justified, in particular an exception indicating the entry into force of certain provisions on the day following their publication and publication. This is due to the need to ensure the continuity of the Court's operation. There is also a provision indicating a longer period of vacathio legis and concerning the newly created Registry of the Constitutional Tribunal. Ladies and gentlemen! Although this law is common, although this law is standard, it has met with many substantive amendments, but unfortunately it is entirely unjustified. I would like to draw attention, for example, to Mr Budka's amendment, which, as the only voice of the discussion, indicated that the title of the law should be amended. It was his substantive amendment. Another amendment is, for example, Mrs Gasiuk-Pihowicz's amendment, which indicates that the date of entry into force of both laws should be postponed until 1 March 2017. The objectives and intentions are clear. The point is that, even after the President of Rzepliński has left, he should elect the President of the Tribunal under the Act of 22 July 2016, in the light of what the Constitutional Tribunal interpreted in its last ruling concerning the Constitutional Tribunal, i.e. not to honour any rules concerning the candidate for President and in any case to have the candidate accepted by the majority of the general assembly. This is, of course, not in line with the wording of the provisions, the clear wording of the provisions, which is why it is absolutely unacceptable to accept such an amendment. In view of the above, the Law and Justice Club will support the law and ask for the rejection of amendments that constitute minority proposals. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u118	Thank you very much. I ask Mr Krzysztof Paszyk to speak on behalf of the Polish People's Party Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u119	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party Allow me to present our position on the report of the committee on the parliamentary draft law Rules introducing a law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Act states that if, by the date of its announcement, the General Assembly of the Constitutional Tribunal has either submitted candidates to the President of the Tribunal or selected candidates in breach of the current Act of 22 July, the procedure for the election of candidates for the President of the Tribunal shall be carried out under the rules laid down in that Act. According to the project, all acts and acts carried out under the procedure of presenting candidates for the position of President of the Republic to the President of the Republic shall be repealed. There is no doubt that this provision is intended to introduce into the judgment of the Judges of December and to prevent judges elected in June of this year from taking office. This is what the laws serve today - to resolve current political disputes. According to the project we are investigating, the judge of the Tribunal may, within one month of the entry into force of this law, make a statement to the President of the Tribunal on the transition to retirement in connection with the introduction of new rules for the performance of the duties of the Judge of the Tribunal during his term of office. In that case, the judge of the Tribunal shall retire on the first day of the month following the month in which the declaration is made. Inevitably, High Izbo, there are associations here with the unceremonious introduction of political corruption, directly encouraging judges to retire in order to make room for the so-called. Yours. With regard to proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, the Act provides for the operation of a new law, i.e. that in accordance with Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the draft in cases initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Organisation and Procedure Act before the Court of Justice, the proceedings shall be conducted in accordance with the rules laid down in that Law. The proceedings before the Court of Justice before the entry into force of the Act on organisation and procedure before the Court of Justice shall remain in force. The project also provides for the reorganisation of the Court's work as of 1 January 2018. The Tribunal's Office will be wound up and the court's law firm and the court's legal service will be established. According to the authors in the explanatory memorandum: Creating a rational organisational structure for support units will encourage savings and reduce annual maintenance costs. Nothing has changed since the committee's work here either. You continue to insist that, by reorganising and setting up a new office, you clean the tribunal of your own employees and replace them with a political key. A completely new institution appears in the draft: the position of the judge acting as President of the Tribunal. It's a very original idea, and it doesn't know its equal anywhere in Europe. In accordance with the law, the President of the Republic shall elect a Judge of the Tribunal who shall be entrusted with the duties of President of the Tribunal, among the judges of the Tribunal with the longest term of office in the judiciary or in the state administration. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u169	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On 30 September 2016, the Government of the Republic of Poland submitted a draft budget law to the Sejm for 2017. The first reading of this project took place on 20 October this year. After the Sejm has failed to accept the motion to reject the draft law at first reading, this draft, in accordance with Article. Article 106 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm were addressed to the Committee on Public Finance and individual parts of the draft were sent to the relevant Sejm committees to submit to the Committee on Public Finance positions containing proposals, opinions or proposals for amendments together with justification. The draft budget law was examined by the Committee on Public Finances in accordance with the timetable adopted by the Sejm Bureau. As a result of this work, a report in print No 1094, accompanied by 242 minority proposals, is presented. Before discussing the work of the Committee on Public Finances on the draft budget, the most important information on the macroeconomic assumptions adopted for the construction of the budget and the basic size of the state budget should be recalled. As regards macro-economic assumptions, which were set several months ago, particularly in terms of growth of 3.6%, they should be defined as ambitious because we have recently seen a slowdown in the pace of economic growth across Europe and around the world. The European Commission has lowered, I will remind the High House, the growth forecasts not only in the European Union but also in the United States. But setting these growth targets as ambitious does not mean that these are not realistic assumptions as announced by the government to accelerate growth from the second quarter of 2017, coupled with an increase in the level of investment, which in turn will involve mobilising European funds from a new budgetary perspective of the European Union. The government, when preparing the draft budget, assumed that in 2016 real GDP growth would be 3.4% , which seemed rational when the European Commission's forecast pointed to GDP growth in Poland in 2016 at 3,7% , whereas from the macroeconomic forecasts of professional Polish forecasts of June this year. it showed that GDP growth should not go beyond the range of 3.2 and 3.7% this year. However, the latest figures from the Central Statistical Office for the three quarters of this year indicate that this year's economic growth will be around 3%, which may, but may not have to affect the results of 2017, because much will depend on world development, including the European economy next year. Problems in the European Union, not only in the Union but also outside it, on a broad scale, global problems related to, among others: with Brexit and the great uncertainty about the policies that will be pursued by the world powers, including the United States in particular after the recent presidential elections, contribute to the deterioration of investment sentiment. It is not surprising, therefore, that not only the European Commission, but also other institutions, including the International Monetary Fund and the OECD, have revised their forecasts for 2017. In the last autumn forecast, the Commission assumes that in 2017 economic growth in the European Union will be 1.6%, i.e. will be 0.3 percentage points lower than expected in the spring of this year In the United States, this increase will be 2.1% And it's going to be 0.1 percentage points lower. In the case of Poland, the Commission forecasts growth in 2017 at 3,4% , i.e. 0.2 percentage points less than expected in the spring forecast. The current projections of the committee are in line with the forecasts of Polish macro-economicists, who in June this year. They assumed that, according to the central scenario in 2017, the national economy would grow at a rate of 3.4%. It should therefore be noted that these forecasts are broadly in line with the 2017 budget. The European Commission's latest forecast also shows that in 2017 Poland will continue to have a faster growth rate than the average in the European Union and in the euro area, with a faster pace than in Poland forecast for only four countries of the European Union, namely Ireland, Luxembourg, Malta and Romania. The inflation rate in Poland will be 1,3% in 2017, so it will be the same as the one established by the Polish government, but slightly lower than in the European Union. Here the predictions are 1.6%. In 2017, investments will increase by 2.5% in the European Union , and in the euro area by 3.1%. In Poland it will be a higher increase, because it is estimated by the committee at 3.7%. Higher investment growth than in Poland is expected to have 13 countries of the European Union, i.e. Estonia, Ireland, Greece, Cyprus, Latvia, Lithuania, Malta, the Netherlands, Slovenia, Slovakia, Croatia, Hungary and Romania. A lower level of investment than in Poland is expected in 11 countries in the Community. The Commission's investment growth will be lower by 2.2 percentage points than that of the government, with a level of 5.9%. Therefore, in order to achieve the expected dynamics of investment, it is necessary first and foremost to make good and efficient use of EU funds from the current financial perspective 2012 The budgetary deficit of the public finance sector will be 1.7% in 2017 in the European Union. GDP, in euro area countries - 1.5% GDP. In Poland it is expected to reach 3% according to the European Commission GDP, and according to the government and the Monetary Policy Council, 2.9%. This means that the deficit will be above the EU average. However, the debt of the public finance sector will amount to more than 85% in 2017 in the European Union. GDP, exactly 85.1% , and more than 90% GDP, 90.6% in the euro area countries and in Poland it will amount to 55% GDP, so it's going to be relatively low. This means that the sustainability of public finances will be preserved in Poland. As regards GDP components, it can be recalled that the government assumes slightly lower export and import dynamics in 2017 than this year, which will keep a secure cash account deficit of 0.8% GDP. At the same time, domestic demand is expected to increase by 4.2%. As to the assumptions adopted by the government for the construction of next year's budget, it should be added that the labour market situation is expected to improve in 2017. The average employment in the national economy is to be 9996 000. people and the unemployment rate recorded at the end of the period is expected to fall to 8%. The average gross remuneration in the national economy will increase by 5% and will amount to PLN 4263 while the average pension paid from the Social Insurance Fund will increase by 2.3% compared to the expected implementation in 2016, i.e. to the level of almost PLN 2057. In turn the average monthly agricultural pension paid from KRUS, i.e. from the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund, will increase to PLN 1083 and the pension - to PLN 1014. In the draft budget submitted to the Sejm for 2017, the government adopted revenue of PLN 325,4 billion, i.e. 2.4% higher compared to expected implementation in 2016. At the same time, the forecast assumes an increase in tax revenue of 9.4%. , which seems to be a real value in relation to the recorded 8% growth in the three quarters of this year compared to the three quarters of last year. The increase in budget revenue will mainly be influenced by the macroeconomic factors previously identified and by measures to rebuild the stream of tax revenue and to seal the tax system through inter alia. introducing a tax avoidance clause, computerising tax books, sealing the system of taxes on goods and services and new solutions to the Gambling Act. In 2017, the share of municipalities in PIT revenues will be increased again to 49,74%. of 49.64% in 2016. State budget expenditure for 2017 has been established on the basis of the so-called. the expenditure rule contained in Article. 112aa Public Finance Act. According to it, an expenditure limit of PLN 384.8 billion was planned, which means an increase of PLN 16.2 billion, i.e. 4.4% in relation to the level adopted in the 2016 Budget Act. Thus, the share of expenditure in GDP will be 19.7% versus 19.8% in 2016. The government draft budget law for 2017 provided inter alia. measures to finance the 500+ family programme - i.e. measures of PLN 23 billion, to reduce the retirement age to 60 years for women and 65 years for men, and to increase from 1 March 2017 the lowest pension to PLN 1000, to guarantee an increase in value, to increase salaries in the national budgetary sphere and average salaries of teachers employed in schools and local government units, to increase the limit on national defence expenditure, including the implementation of the multiannual programme, to secure the air transport of the most important persons in the country, to infrastructure for land, road and rail transport, including the National Road Construction Programme for 2014-2023, with a view to 2025 and the National Railway Programme by 2023 - in this area there will be an increase in expenditure of more than PLN 2.1 billion, to continue restructuring tasks in the hard coal mining sector, to finance free medicines and medical products for persons who have completed 75 years of life, to compensate for agricultural and livestock insurance payments and to start up the scheme. In addition, the draft budget provides for the continuation of financing of other tasks undertaken in previous years, such as increasing access to pre-school education, financing of regulations under the Law on Free Legal Aid and Legal Education, securing funds for subsidies to agricultural fuels, protecting the recipient of sensitive electricity or financing the specialisation of doctors within the residency of all medical graduates. In summary, it is worth noting that the draft budget for 2017 will reflect the implementation of government priorities and electoral commitments and will provide funding for the basic functions of the state while maintaining the sustainability of public finances. [[Emotions will be there.]] I will now present the work of the Committee on Public Finance to the High House. This work in the finance committee on the draft budget took place from 20 October to 1 December 2016 in accordance with the agreed timetable. At its meetings, the Commission considered its own budgetary parts and the opinions of the other Sejm committees within the scope of the order of the Marshal of the Sejm of 6 October this year. During the meetings of the Committee on Public Finance, opinions were presented on the sections of the budget considered, as well as the opinions of representatives of other committees and correspondents on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance. Discussions were held, questions were asked and answers were given by government representatives and budget managers. In some cases, it was necessary to reply in writing. Members also received the opinion of the Sejm Analysis Office. The Sejm committees submitted a total of 26 opinions to the Committee on Public Finance, with the vast majority of which did not raise any objections to the draft budget law by accepting government submissions. At the same time, the joint committees examining the budget of the Main Office of Measures proposed that the Committee on Public Finances should take into account the financial demands of the President of this institution, which indicated that the implementation of the tasks of the Main Office of Measurement and the planned changes, which would be intended to transform the current office into a modern metrology institute, would require an increase in the budget of the Main Office of Measures for 2017 by a total of over PLN 13 million. Of this amount, 9420 thousand. would be intended for investment purposes, while 3820 thousand. for salaries for newly employed specialists and highly rated employees. It should also be added that the Committee on Culture and Media, in a positive opinion on the draft budget of the National Council for Radiophony and Television, expressed concern about a very significant increase, because by more than 35%, expenditure in 2017 and thus an increase in employment by 60 posts, i.e. by several dozen percent. In this context, the committee has required the National Council to examine the proposals so far and to present a more realistic proposal to the Committee on Public Finance so that these increases are lower while safeguarding the core tasks of the National Radio and Television Council. On the other hand, the Committee on Justice and Human Rights indicated in its opinion that it had given a positive opinion on the draft budget law for 2017, excluding Part 06: the Constitutional Tribunal, with which it decided to abstain until the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal Resolution of 15 September 2016 on the adoption of the draft revenue and expenditure of the Constitutional Tribunal in 2017. This issue raised a discussion at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finances, but it should be noted that the Committee on Public Finances in budgetary matters is not closely linked to the opinions of other committees, both negative and positive. Therefore, after examining the draft budget of the Court and the opinion of the Coreferent, she accepted the submitted budget of the Court without making any changes to it. The Commission's expectation of a review of the resolutions by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in order to be able to comment on the proposed budget of the Constitutional Tribunal could mean, in practice, that the work on the examination of the draft budget law should be suspended and the committee does not have the right to do so. It shall be followed by a timetable for the work on the government draft budget law drawn up by the Bureau of the Sejm, as well as constitutional deadlines for the adoption of the budget law. A total of 291 amendments were tabled to the draft budget law for 2017 at first reading of the budget, including three on the text section. Of the 288 amendments relating to the annexes to the budget, 98 amendments concerned the revenue of the State budget set out in Annex 1 and indicated the need to raise tax revenue in order to finance specific expenditure. 185 amendments concerned the expenditure of the State budget included in Annex 2 and were mainly amendments reducing the special reserves and measures foreseen for the operation of the Treasury debt, including the Institute of National Remembrance for the financing of various regional objectives. One amendment concerned Annex 6: Salaries in state budget units and four amendments aimed at amending the financial plans of State special purpose funds and public legal persons, Annexes 13 and 14. The committees tabled 7 amendments and two separate sentences, while MEPs tabled 282 amendments, most of which were tabled by the Civic and Modern Platform clubs. During the votes, 7 amendments were withdrawn, including two on the text part, and only 22 amendments were accepted by the Committee on Public Finance. In the light of the amendments adopted, it should be pointed out that the Committee on Public Finance has adopted a parliamentary amendment to increase the level of the general reserve by 121 080 000. zł by reducing the expenditure of a number of budgetary parts whose dispatchers, in accordance with Article. Article 139 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Laws on public finances, have so-called. budgetary autonomy, and by reducing expenditure of the Ministry of Justice. In Part 07: The Supreme Audit Chamber reduced expenditure by 2774 thousand. zł, i.e. as proposed in the opinion of the Commission for State Control. PLN and property expenditure - by 559 thousand. zł. Part 09: National Radio and Television Council reduced expenditure by 7808 thousand. PLN, of which current expenditure of budgetary units - by 7 million and property expenditure - by 808 thousand. zł. In Part 10: The General Data Protection Officer reduced the current expenditure of budgetary units by 3823 000. zł. In Part 11: National Election Office reduced property expenditure by 1508 thousand. zł. Part 12: The State Labour Inspection reduced the current expenditure of the budget units by 15 147 000. zł. In Part 13: Institute of National Remembrance - Commission for the Investigation of Crimes Against the Polish Nationals reduced expenditure by 36 587 thousand. zł, including: in the general offices of state authorities, control and protection of the law, Chapter 75101, current expenditure of budgetary units - by 16 500 thousand. Since this amendment raised various reservations from Members of the opposition clubs, four points should be pointed out. Firstly, that in the past the proposals for expenditure submitted by the budgetary autonomy bodies have also been reduced by the Sejm, sometimes quite substantially, because the expenditure proposed by these entities, in accordance with the applicable law, is not previously corrected by the government or the Minister of Finance. Secondly, after the increase, the total reserve will amount to 241,081 thousand. zł, i.e. twice the amount from the government submission, but it will amount to 0.06% State budget expenditure and will therefore continue to be well below the limit of the Acceptable Public Finance Act of 0,2% State budget expenditure. Thirdly, it can be recalled that a general reserve of nearly PLN 200 million, representing 0.06%, is planned in the 2015 budget law. the total expenditure of the State budget. This means that the increased overall reserve as a result of the amendment is relatively the same as in 2015. Fourthly, the proposed reductions in the expenditure of entities with budgetary autonomy cannot be considered to be too far-reaching and not permitting tasks to be carried out, as after the reductions made: the expenditure of the Supreme Court will be 6.5% higher than the expenditure planned for 2016; the expenditure of the Supreme Audit Chamber will be 4.1% higher; the expenditure of the Ombudsman will be 4.4% higher , and the Ombudsman - 1.3%; expenditure of the National Radio and Television Council will be higher by 3.5% , the General Data Protection Officer - by 8.2%; the expenditure of the State Labour Inspection will be higher by 1.1%; the expenditure of the National Judicial Board after the reduction resulting from this amendment, as well as the next amendment, will be higher by 7% than planned for 2016 and the expenditure of the Supreme Administrative Court after the reductions resulting from the two subsequent amendments will be higher by 5%. In the case of the National Election Office and the Ministry of Justice, the dispatchers themselves planned lower expenditure for 2017 than for the current year, i.e. 2016, and the adopted amendment reduces their level slightly compared to the government's submission, as only about 2%. Therefore, the reductions made on the one hand should not jeopardise the performance of the tasks of all these entities and on the other would allow for a level of general reserve that would enable the government or the finance minister to act flexibly in unexpected situations. To date, in disaster situations, the State has helped farmers through a public support system related to the assessment of losses by committees appointed by voivodships at the level of each municipality. This mechanism is universally - I stress: it is universally criticised by farmers as an inconsistent, dishonest mechanism based on imprecise estimates, and at the same time generating huge costs for the state budget. The next few years show that it will not be possible to modernise, modernise, modify this system. Whether it's a dream or a rainstorm or a hurricane or a drought - it's always some kind of discretion for those who judge, often in good faith, but because they don't have an appropriate cognitive, research workshop, it's very difficult for them to determine what's caused by disaster and what's caused by, for example. errors of agrotechnics, inadequate pH or lack of micronutrients to give these few elementary factors affecting the condition of the plants. Therefore, like other countries that care about their agriculture, especially the United States, we are introducing a general system of crop and animal insurance on farms, but so designed that farmers can afford to pay part of the contribution so that it does not turn out that these contributions, expected or demanded by insurance companies, insurance companies, are so high that farmers simply cannot afford these contributions. In the following years this amount will increase to reach PLN 1650 million in 2020, which is already quite distant. Higher education and science have a key influence on the civilization of Poland and socio-economic development. In November 2015, 1.4 million students studied in Poland. The number of students since 2005 when nearly 2 million students were studying at Polish universities is still decreasing. In 2011, 1.8 million students had already studied, which accounts for 40.8% persons aged between 19 and 24 years. 20 years earlier, this rate was only 9.8%. In the academic year 2014/2015, the number of students was 1.47 million and the university's diploma gained 395 thousand. persons. The positive phenomenon in Polish higher education is the increase in the number of foreigners studying in Poland. According to the latest data, the number of foreign students is 57 thousand and the year before that was only 46 thousand. people from abroad. This year, 31 thousand students study in Poland. The quantitative development of higher education, including research and teaching infrastructure, should be accompanied to a greater extent by positive qualitative developments. The proportions of the number of students in fixed and permanent studies and different degrees of study should be closely adapted to the needs of the economy, and the study programmes and forms of teaching must keep pace with world trends so that Polish universities can find a more worthy place in world rankings. In addition to the quantitative changes in the 2017 budget, which I will talk about later, qualitative changes are being prepared in the area of higher education and science. The Ministry of Science and Higher Education is preparing a programme for the construction of local zones for discovering active knowledge. It will be a kind of minicentres of Copernicus Science Centre, i.e. local district science centers equipped with modern laboratories. It is in these local science centres that children's universities and polytechnics, universities of the third century, and above all in these laboratories will be able to hold local school lessons, especially in the field of natural sciences. This project will be implemented by 2021 and will create up to 100 such zones for discovering active knowledge. In addition to building minicentres with laboratories where experimental activities can take place, the Ministry of Science and Higher Education plans to create a centre of science in VR technology, i.e. virtual reality. This concept predicts that anyone will be able to download an application from the Internet, which will allow using a smartphone and additional glasses to immerse themselves in virtual reality and gain knowledge there. The Ministry plans to allocate to this end to PLN 150 million by 2021. At the same time, the Ministry is implementing a strategy that includes three pillars: a package of changes for science and higher education, building bridges between science and the economy and social responsibility of science. In the area of legal changes, since 1 October, a deregulation law has entered into force reducing bureaucracy at universities. The implementation of this law varies from one university to another, which concerns the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. Since 1 January 2017, a new funding algorithm for higher education institutions is to enter into force in which a number of pro-quality criteria have been introduced. During the proceedings of the Committee on Public Finances, there were voices that this algorithm could be severe for some universities, e.g. for polytechnics that educate graduates who are mostly employed immediately after graduating. The Ministry's work on the Law on Higher Education is also ongoing, so-called. Law 2.0. The draft law is widely consulted in the academic community. The Ministry of Science and Higher Education wants the new law to define the future of science not only for the coming years but for decades. In terms of cooperation between science and the economy, the Ministry concluded a public consultation on the implementation of the academic career path. Since spring 2017, it is expected that PhDs will be available not only on the basis of theoretical work but also on the basis of economic implementation. The Ministry proposes in a modified version a concept that the implementation path also includes habilitation and the possibility of obtaining the title of professor. In the area of the third pillar in the so-called. The Government's strategy, i.e. the social responsibility of science, provides for a strong increase in investment in the development of third-century universities. This is supposed to be one of the greatest phenomena of bottom-up social self-organization. This is civil society in action, as Deputy Prime Minister Jarosław Gowin said at the last conference. The Ministry of Science and Higher Education's strategy for the reform of the higher education and science area assumes inter alia. The creation of new research universities, which will be the driving force of Poland in the world scientific race. Two new agendas are planned: the National Institute of Technology to deal with the transfer of knowledge to the economy, and the Academic Exchange Agenda, which will deal with international scientific policy. The aim of this strategy is to successfully increase the R &amp; D co-financing rate from the current level of 0.87% GDP to more than 1% in order to reach a target of 1.91% in the EU countries GDP. And only in 2017, we estimate very carefully that it will amount to PLN 16 million, 66 million in 2018, 135 million in 2019 and about 230 million in the following years. What is cost savings, and how is it possible to increase the number of teachers' jobs? This is due to a simple conversion factor. As regards the financing of the reform that we are currently preparing, education reforms, I would like to say that, in addition to the money that is secured in the budget, there is also money in other places that can be reached to properly prepare for the reform. These are funds for the financing of the own tasks of local government units and are also secured funds in this reserve. Another reserve, the general reserve in Part 81, is the appropriations for the tasks planned for the planning phase. They are planned to amount to 120 million, where they can also reach for measures to prepare for the reform of education. In addition to national measures, please countries, there are also EU funds. When it comes to funds that are intended for voivodships, it is over PLN 30 billion. Ladies and gentlemen, as part of infrastructure expenditure, expenditure may be considered eligible, inter alia. Of course, for educational infrastructure, for the provision of school or educational system facilities with teaching aids and ICT tools necessary for the implementation of curricula in schools or educational systems, including the provision of adequate network and service infrastructure. EU funds can also be used to equip schoolrooms with learning tools e.g. natural objects or mathematics. They can also be used to equip schools or educational establishments with teaching aids and specialist equipment to identify developmental, educational and psychophysical needs and support development, provide therapy to pupils with special educational needs, as well as school textbooks and teaching materials adapted to the needs of pupils with disabilities, with particular regard to those aids, equipment and tools that are compatible with the concept of universal design. They can also be used to adapt existing pre-school education sites to the needs of children with disabilities, as well as to equip schools and institutions of the vocational education system with equipment and teaching aids for teaching in a specific sector, as well as for modernisation and adaptation of existing infrastructure, in particular the extension or reconstruction, and, where appropriate, the construction of workshops, practical training workshops, as well as the workshops used in the training and development of key competences, oriented to the needs of the regional labour market, and the equipment of workshops and workshops guaranteeing high quality of education. During this period, the correct calculation of the costs of carrying out these tasks was not secured. The introduction of standardisation for all tasks is, of course, time-consuming, labour-intensive and follows gradually, taking into account tasks or maybe starting with tasks that are the most common in terms of their performance by local authorities, taking the most time away from these local authorities, and generating significant consequences. Of course, I am talking here about those tasks which are not explicitly included in the legislation of costs, such as those relating to family benefits or educational benefits introduced this year, where the legislator immediately provided for a specific way of measuring the costs of this task. However, with regard to other costs, such standardisation shall take place. Already two years ago, civil affairs were standardized, but also in 2016 there was another stage of standardization of these tasks in the field of real estate management, where the scope of the tasks to be carried out in carrying out these tasks commissioned from the government administration was defined. They will then be settled and financed in this way, as a wage for man-hours has also been set in the performance of these tasks. I would also like to remind you that the tasks assigned, as the name itself indicates, are tasks which are carried out for government administration, and the corresponding costs financed by the state budget concern tasks rather than persons. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u479	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! According to Art. 177 of the Public Finance Act, which states that, in the event of delays in the performance of tasks or the excess of funds available, it may be decided to block planned budget expenditure, I would like to propose a revision of the Budget Act as well as of the Around Budget Act for 2016. In the course of the implementation of the budget, the so-called. natural savings, which are among others. the result of actions and objective factors, e.g. result from the outcome of the tendering procedures or from changes in the timetables for the implementation of the tasks. The expenditure blocked in this way may be transferred to the special-purpose reserve for the State Treasury's obligations or for the purposes indicated separately in the Budget Act. This amendment to the budget law aims to enable the Minister for Development and Finance to set up two main objectives from these blocked resources in 2016. The first is the purchase of cultural goods, including monuments and rights to cultural objects of particular importance for the Polish state and cultural heritage. And the second - possible special grant to the Material Reserve Agency for the implementation of strategic reserve tasks. The accompanying budget amendment to the Around Budget Act regulates the mode and timing of the allocation of these reserves. Such a modification is necessary in view of the timetable for the launch of these measures and the forthcoming end of the year. The reserve allocation shall be carried out by the Minister for Development and Finance in agreement with the other budget managers. Nor will it be mandatory for the Sejm committee responsible for the budget to give an opinion on this division and, similarly, the opinion of the committee will not be necessary to amend the plan of the Material Reserve Agency. This is not the case, for God's sakes, because of the intention of skipping the role of the committee in deciding on the allocation of funds, but, as indicated, it follows that the end of the year is near and that it is the only way to mobilise funds in time and that the objectives are indicated by the budget law. So to sum up, the two main objectives are that we have a budget deficit in default, which is a surplus for this, and in particular in the second case, the Material Reserve Agency - it is not known whether it will be activated. The aim is to create the possibility of launching, before the end of the year in the mode provided for by the Act, to mobilise funds for strategic reserves as set out in the Act on the Material Reserves Agency. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u619	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Joint Committee on Energy and the Treasury and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, I have the honour to submit to the High House a report of the committee on the governmental draft law on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland in print No 1055. The aim of the proposed regulation is to establish the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland on the basis of the achievements of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the General Treasury, as well as the Acting Prosecutor's Office of the Second Republic of Poland, the Public Prosecutor's Office of the General Kingdom of Poland, the Galician Public Prosecutor's Office of the Treasury and the achievements of other European countries in which specialized bodies of state attorneys operate. The purpose of the General Prosecutor's Office as a highly specialised institutional representative is to maximise the security and effectiveness of the protection of the rights and interests of the Republic of Poland, including the Treasury and the State Treasury and non-State property belonging to the State Treasury, in particular in proceedings before courts, tribunals and other judicial authorities. The objectives set according to the proposed Act of the Public Prosecutor General of the Republic of Poland will be implemented, among others. by consolidating and improving the rules for the implementation of the replacement of the Republic of Poland, the Treasury, government administrations, state organisational units without legal personality, the creation of a professional analytical and procedural facilities, the power to develop recommendations and designs and the opinion of legal acts, including the mandatory assessment of actions, if the value of the subject of the legal activity assessed exceeds the amount of PLN 100 million. Another important task of the Public Prosecutor's Office is the opinion of normative acts concerning the essential rights or interests of the Republic of Poland, including the State Treasury, the creation of institutional facilities for the Council of Ministers and other entities representing the State Treasury, the settlement of disputes and the creation of a procedure for the resolution of disputes by the Public Prosecutor's Office in the field of the judicial representation of the State Treasury. The Act sets out in detail the powers and organisation of the Public Prosecutor's Office, the Office of the Public Prosecutor's Office, as well as the role of the President and Vice-Presidents of the Public Prosecutor's Office. The supervisory powers of the President of the Council of Ministers on the Public Prosecutor General and the President of the Public Prosecutor General correspond with the political power of the Polish Parliament to perform control functions. The Act requires the President of the General Prosecutor's Office to report annually to the Sejm on its activities and on the activities of the General Prosecutor's Office for the previous calendar year. The Commission has adopted several amendments concerning, inter alia, rules for the interaction of the Polish Public Prosecutor's Office with public authorities, the level of fees paid by persons replaced and the remuneration paid to advisers and referenda. Joint committees: the Energy and Treasury Commission and the Committee on Justice and Human Rights recommend the draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

If the ownership function is not centralised, the minimum requirement is to establish a strong coordination body from among different administrative units. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u681	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The Treasury Property Management Act is a law which does not interfere with the powers of entities other than those entrusted with the property management function. We rely on internationally recognized designs. We have indeed been inspired by the OECD guidelines, creating a structure based on a strong coordination centre with the President of the Council of Ministers. However, in order not to become dogmatist, we assumed that it was desirable to allow the exercise of rights from actions to individual departments, as is accepted, among others. in the Federal Republic of Germany. This model works there. We assume that it will also work with us. All standards that have been in force so far are maintained and are usually raised, e.g. The agreement of the Council of Ministers to dispose of the shares, which is now becoming a rule and has so far been an exception. At the same time, we are introducing a modern model of regulation that is adapted to the commercial law system. There will be no more situations where the sale of one share, as in the case of Xcity, resulted in the company being excluded from the scope of control over the management of the State Treasury. This is a company that trades millions. The proposed law is also an effective tool to fight the pathologies associated with the management of the State Treasury property. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u694	Thank you very much. That concludes our parliamentary statements.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u695	A statement by the Members on the 128th anniversary of the birth of General Stanisław Rostworski. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! December 19th this year We will celebrate the 128th anniversary of the birth of General Stanisław Rostworski, a soldier of Polish Legions, the Polish Army and the National Army awarded the Golden Cross and the Silver Cross of the Order of War Virtuti Militari, the Cross of Independence, as well as four times the Cross of Valor, the Cross of Military Merit and posthumously the Cross of the National Army. Stanisław Rostworski was born into an aristocratic family. He was the son of the painter Stanisław Jakub Nałęcz and Teresa, the Countess of the House of Lubieniecki. He studied at the University of Switzerland, where he obtained a PhD in agricultural chemistry, as well as at the Jagiellonian University, where he obtained a PhD in philosophy. During World War I Rostworowski participated in the creation of Polish Legions, then joined the 2nd Ulyan Squadron. He took part in fights, among others. under Molotkov and Sokołówka. Then in 1915 he joined the second legionary brigade and took part with other units in the campaign in Lublin. During the maneuvers of the transition to Volyn, he obtained the rank of lieutenant and was then assigned to the rank of Chief of Staff of the Second Brigade. A year later it was cancelled from the front and assigned to military publications of the Military Department of the National Committee in Piotrków. A few months later, he became a colonel's aide. Sikorsky and got the rank of lieutenant. It was then incorporated into the Polish Armed Forces in Galicia. After graduating from the officer's school, he became a member of the National Train Inspector's staff. When Władysław Sikorski's initiative set up a Regency Board, Rostworski was adopted as a member of the Regency Board of Prince Zdzisław Lubomirski. At the end of World War I, he was appointed captain. In the 20th interwar period Rostworowski performed several different functions in the Polish Army. In 1931, he was promoted to a diplomat colonel, and four years later he devoted himself to social and scientific activities. He returned to the army after the outbreak of World War II. He participated in the defense of Warsaw and commanded Kalisz's anti-aircraft defense. He managed to avoid slavery and went to Paris to report to Władysław Sikorski. He was then transferred to Bucharest and after 2 years to Budapest. The Germans closed Polish diplomatic posts in Hungary, so only conspiracy activities were possible. At the beginning of May 1942. Rostworowski entered Warsaw and adopted the nickname Odra at the AK headquarters. He was assigned to a group of senior officers for special assignments. Then he became Brigadier General. He served as an inspector in the area of Krakow and Silesia. Together with other officers, he was to inspect guerrilla troops, field troops, as well as conduct trainings of branch commanders. 1.5 A year later he became the boss of the so-called. the observatory. After the outbreak of the Warsaw Uprising, he took over management in the area of Kraków, Silesia and Zaolzia. August 11th at 3 p.m. 6 a.m. was detained by the Gestapo and transported to the police headquarters. There General Stanisław Rostworowski was murdered, most likely on the night of 11 to 12 August 1944. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u696	I am pleased to inform you that at the request of the President of the Institute of National Remembrance Dr Jarosław Szark, the President of the Republic of Poland of 29 July 2016, Mr Jan Naskręk, the resident of Prudnik and a long-standing member of the Prudnice County Law and Justice, was awarded the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity. Mr Jan Naskręt is a true patriot for whom free Poland was the highest value. Jan Naskręk was born on May 23, 1935 in Socbork. He graduated from high school in Prudnik. He was an electrical installer by profession. He started his anti-communist activity in June 1980. In October, a Territorial Commission was set up at the Craft of Various Crafts, where he became the first chairman. He was then appointed to the Inter-Business Committee on Solidarity in Prudnik. On 1 April 1981, a rap sheet was established for anti-communist activities and statements against the communist regime. On 15 December 1981, Jan Naskręt was first detained by the Opole Security Service. They wanted to force him to sign the so-called. Loyalties. Despite pressure from the SB, he did not agree to this, he was harassed, imprisoned, among others. on ul. Traugutta in Prudnik. In January of the same year he was again arrested for so-called. to cause public unrest. In March 1982, he was sentenced by the District Court of Opole to 2 years of imprisonment for 4 years and supervision of the curator. Two years later, in 1984, he was arrested again and the security services searched his apartment. The reason for the arrest was too active activity in the structures of NSZZ (Solidarity), dissemination of information against apparatus of repression and possession of leaflets boycotting the body of the Polish People's Republic. In 1986, the apartment was searched again. Jan Naskręk was arrested for 24 hours at the time. The same year, the Interim Council of the NSZZ Region was established.Solidarity of Silesia of Opole was established. At one of the meetings in Nysa on the street. Prusa 2 five members of the Council were detained and sent to the College for union activities. In 1989, he became the President of the Committee of the Civil Land of Prudnica. This committee won in the local elections. At one of the sessions, Jan Naskręk applied for the removal of the Soviet monument from the city centre. This proposal was supported by a total of 19 aldermen. The mayor passed the resolution to the applicant, Jan Naskręt, who himself removed the monument from the Liberty Square in Prudnik. In 1990, he became head of the Civil Affairs Department. His initiative was among others. launching kitchens for the needy. This kitchen supplied 250 people and operated for the next 12 years. Mr. Jan Naskręt is a man of honor, valour and faith. His attitude and anti-communist activities deserved a great distinction. Such a distinction is undoubtedly the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity. In the hearts of noble misfortune and humiliation of the country is always the source of patriotism. - Józef Piłsudski.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u697	Declaration on the need to improve the economic knowledge of Poles. Today's statement I would like to devote to a very important issue, which is the level of economic knowledge held by Poles. Global Financial Education Survey Standard & Poor's 42% Poles understand basic concepts of finance, such as inflation or compound interest. Unfortunately, it is not better to know about the principles of social security contributions. It's almost 60% here. He doesn't know anything about the social security system, only 10% has a satisfactory level of knowledge, as shown by recent studies. Many people also confuse different types of insurance or do not distinguish e.g. Health sickness insurance does not know the provisions relating to the payment of contributions from different types of contracts. Such ignorance later has very often negative effects, for example. It may be easier for a dishonest employer to deceive an employee. Particular emphasis on the need to improve the level of economic knowledge should be placed on primary and secondary pupils. Young people need to be made aware of the obligation and the need to pay contributions, as there are many young people who take up work in black without any insurance. The introduction of education on this issue will contribute to increasing economic and financial awareness. Today, there is a curriculum for teaching the basics of entrepreneurship in schools, but it is implemented at different levels and, according to experts, it does not provide sufficient knowledge of the basics of economics. By making a statement on the state of economic awareness, I would like it to be a clue, as well as a clue to what can be improved by preparing a reform of education, so as to raise the level of economic knowledge starting with primary school education.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-13-sejm-32-1.u698	Statement on consumer awareness and economic patriotism. Despite what they're trying to tell us today's media, media, patriotism isn't a negative thing. Or a backroom. Also economic patriotism. However, the fourth authority forgets to add that Polish entrepreneurs and consumers have been faced with economic racism for years rather than trying to create decent conditions for doing business and enjoying its fruits. Consumer awareness has also been seriously affected as a result of such treatment. us - citizens of the Republic of Poland. The truth is that betting on Polish products brings double benefits. Firstly, we use the products of the highest class and proven brand, and secondly, we give a powerful impetus to the development of our national economy. The trend of excluding foreign goods from the domestic market for the benefit of domestic products is also very strong in many European countries, but not only. Especially recently. Which is encouraging - this trend in no way misses Poland either. The consumer consciousness of Poles is in an increasingly better condition. The above statement is confirmed by numerous scientific studies. Efforts to prove that this thesis is true have been multiplied and intensified especially recently. Analyzing the progressive trends on the basis of research commissioned by the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, we must note that in 2007 more than half of Poles did not like to shop. It was a duty of necessity, and the reasons for such a situation could be seen in many aspects, but a difficult material situation was the decisive one. As it turns out, nearly 80% then the units examined considered the price of the product concerned as having the greatest impact on the decision to make the transaction. In particular, this problem concerned older people who were supplied with only the first-needed products. For research purposes, they were called "removal consumers'. They accounted for as much as 15% the total number of persons surveyed. On the other hand, only a small group of people were driven by factors such as product quality, product brand or composition and production method. At that time it was the domain of mostly young people, well educated and in a good material situation. According to the report "Mode for Polishness," which was prepared for the purpose of the project "Consumer 2016" by TNS Polska, and was published relatively recently, because in March 2016, the above trends and habits are completely reversed. The results can be surprising because it turns out that up to 89% The respondents claimed that the fact that the product was manufactured in Poland is a very important factor determining the conclusion of the transaction. What's more, the labels of certain products marked with inscriptions: "Made in the local company, produced only from organic ingredients, "Made in the family business," "incentives 70%, 65%" and 65% subjects. Still a lot, but definitely less, because in turn 54% and 42% The surveyed feel encouraged to buy labeled products: "Made by a world-famous brand and "Made in the European Union." It is a good forecast that gives hope for a better tomorrow for the state of the Polish national economy. It is worth noting that this trend seems to be only endemic, and in the near future the first products will be definitely native.
